Deep learning for the design of photonic structures
page=1 block=66847680 (x=2 y=60) width=510.000000 height=16.000000 fontname=Whitney-Semibold wordint.min=5 wordint.max=7
Wei Ma? ?1,2, Zhaocheng Liu? ?3,4, Zhaxylyk A. Kudyshev5,6,7,8, Alexandra Boltasseva? ?5,6,7??, 
page=1 block=59114528 (x=2 y=96) width=451.000000 height=11.000000 fontname=Whitney-Semibold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Innovative approaches and tools play an important role in shaping design, characterization and optimization for the field of pho-
page=1 block=67117183 (x=2 y=141) width=510.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Whitney-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tonics. As a subset of machine learning that learns multilevel abstraction of data using hierarchically structured layers, deep 
page=1 block=67117183 (x=2 y=152) width=512.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Whitney-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
learning offers an efficient means to design photonic structures, spawning data-driven approaches complementary to conven-
page=1 block=67117183 (x=2 y=162) width=510.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Whitney-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tional physics- and rule-based methods. Here, we review recent progress in deep-learning-based photonic design by providing 
page=1 block=67117183 (x=2 y=173) width=512.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Whitney-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the historical background, algorithm fundamentals and key applications, with the emphasis on various model architectures for 
page=1 block=67117183 (x=2 y=183) width=512.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Whitney-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
specific photonic tasks. We also comment on the challenges and perspectives of this emerging research direction.

page=1 block=34344594 (x=2 y=746) width=262.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=Whitney-Book wordint.min=1 wordint.max=3

page=1 block=27788496 (x=2 y=112) width=212.000000 height=11.000000 fontname=Whitney-Semibold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=2
New  photonic  structures,  materials,  devices  and  systems 
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=222) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
have been driving forces for transformative technologies, 
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=232) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
including  high-speed  optical  communication  and  com-
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=243) width=250.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
puting, ultrasensitive biochemical detection, efficient solar energy 
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=253) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
harvesting and super-resolution imaging, as well as quantum infor-
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=264) width=250.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
mation processing. Over the past three decades, we have witnessed 
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=274) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tremendous progress in and success of artificially engineered pho-
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=285) width=250.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tonic structures, including photonic crystals, metamaterials  and 
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=295) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
plasmonic nanostructures, with unparalleled capabilities in tailor-
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=306) width=250.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ing light–matter interactions and unlocking new device concepts. 
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=316) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Many fundamental laws have been revisited or generalized in these 
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=327) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
structured media, and consequently they promise a wide range of 
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=337) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
important applications. For instance, photonic crystals can real-
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=348) width=250.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ize complete photonic bandgaps, so that light can transmit around 
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=358) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
a sharp bend surrounded by such crystals with near-perfect effi-
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=369) width=250.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ciency1. Metamaterials demonstrate exceptional properties through 
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=379) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
rational structural designs, exemplified by negative refractive indi-
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=390) width=250.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ces: these enable light to be refracted to the negative direction, in 
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=400) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
contrast to normal refraction based on Snell’s law2–4. By using metal-
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=410) width=250.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
lic nanoparticles with different sizes, geometries and compositions, 
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=421) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
plasmonics can break the classical diffraction limit, offering the 
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=432) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
opportunity to control light emission at the single-molecule level5.
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=442) width=245.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Whether we are discussing individual  plasmonic  nanostruc-
page=1 block=16609644 (x=13 y=453) width=238.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tures, or metamaterials and photonic crystals composed of arrays 
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=463) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of dielectric or metallic building blocks, structural designs play a 
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=474) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
central role. So far, there are two main design approaches. First, we 
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=484) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
can resort to physics-based methods, such as simplified analytical 
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=495) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
models, knowledge obtained from prior or related practice, and sci-
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=505) width=250.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
entific intuition. For example, dielectric and metallic nanoparticles 
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=516) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
with simple geometries (such as spheres, cylinders and core–shell 
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=526) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
particles) can be accurately modelled by Mie theory6. The scatter-
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=536) width=250.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ing, absorption and extinction responses of the particles arise from 
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=547) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
their electric and magnetic multipolar resonances. The initial idea 
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=558) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of split-ring resonators, widely used in the metamaterials commu-
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=568) width=250.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
nity to produce effective magnetism, was based on electromagnetics 
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=579) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and electrical circuit theory7. Specifically, varying external magnetic 
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=589) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
fields induce a current loop and thus a magnetic dipole, which is 
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=600) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
greatly enhanced around the resonance frequency determined by 
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=610) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the internal capacitance and inductance of the resonator. For pho-
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=621) width=250.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tonic crystals, the intuition originated from the successful under-
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=631) width=250.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
standing of electron transport in the periodic potential well of a 
page=1 block=16609644 (x=1 y=642) width=252.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
FOCUS | Review ARticle
page=1 block=-34668512 (x=287 y=1) width=225.000000 height=14.000000 fontname=Whitney-Medium wordint.min=4 wordint.max=6
FOCUS | Review ARticle-y
page=1 block=-34668512 (x=279 y=19) width=233.000000 height=14.000000 fontname=Whitney-Medium wordint.min=4 wordint.max=6
solid material8. In an analogue, light transmission through periodi-
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=221) width=249.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
cally modulated refractive indices can be greatly modified, enabling 
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=232) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
‘photonic semiconductors’ with complete bandgaps that disallow 
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=243) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the propagation of light in the ‘forbidden band. Although these 
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=253) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
physics-based approaches offer important guidelines, it is not trivial 
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=264) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
to find the right structures to realize the desired photonic proper-
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=274) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ties, especially when the geometry and spatial arrangements of the 
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=285) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
structure become complicated.
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=295) width=114.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Therefore, we have to rely on the second approach: electromag-
page=1 block=31355039 (x=274 y=306) width=238.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
netic modelling based on numerical simulation methods such as the 
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=316) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
finite-difference time-domain method, the finite-element method, 
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=327) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the finite integration technique or the method of moments, with 
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=337) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
or without optimization algorithms. Generally, starting from cer-
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=348) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tain initial and boundary conditions, these computational electro-
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=358) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
magnetics  simulations solve the design  problem by  discretizing 
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=369) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Maxwell’s equations spatially and temporally. By setting up suf-
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=379) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ficient meshes and iteration steps, we can accurately calculate the 
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=390) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
optical properties of a given structure. Nevertheless, we often need 
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=400) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
to fine-tune the geometry and iteratively perform simulations to 
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=411) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
gradually approach the targeted responses. This procedure largely 
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=421) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
relies on past experience of the design templates, and owing to con-
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=432) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
straints on simulation power and time, only limited design param-
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=442) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
eters are adjusted in searching for the optimal structure.
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=453) width=207.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
The inverse  design problem, meaning the direct retrieval of 
page=1 block=31355039 (x=274 y=463) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the proper structure for the desired optical performance, requires 
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=474) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
exploration  of  a much larger degree of freedom in the  design 
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=484) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
space, and hence is even more challenging. To search the formi-
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=495) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
dably large design space efficiently, the inverse design procedure is 
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=505) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
usually guided by optimization algorithms: either gradient-based 
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=516) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
approaches (for example, topology optimization, adjoint method 
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=526) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
or level-set method) or evolutionary approaches (such as genetic 
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=537) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
algorithms or particle swarm algorithms). Such inverse design algo-
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=547) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
rithms enable one to find non-intuitive, irregularly shaped pho-
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=558) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tonic structures that outperform empirically designed structures in 
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=568) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
many applications, such as silicon photonic components, photonic 
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=579) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
crystals and metamaterials. Fundamentally, these algorithms are 
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=589) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
rule-based  approaches containing  iterative searching steps in  a 
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=600) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
case-by-case manner, often relying on numerical simulations in 
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=610) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
each step to produce intermediate results that help to modify the 
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=621) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
searching strategy. Such stochastic algorithms are limited by their 
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=631) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
random-search nature and hence are insufficient for complex design 
page=1 block=31355039 (x=263 y=642) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
77

78
page=2 block=1576549 (x=3 y=737) width=8.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=Whitney-Bold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=16
in a multi-constrained problem. Readers interested in inverse design 
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=32) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
in photonics can refer to recent reviews on this topic9–11.
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=42) width=206.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Deep learning allows a computational model composed of mul-
page=2 block=49361248 (x=14 y=53) width=238.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tiple layers of processing units to learn multiple levels of abstrac-
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=64) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tion in given data12. In light of its exceptional success in domains 
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=74) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
related to computer science and engineering, including computer 
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=85) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
vision13, natural language processing14, speech recognition15, knowl-
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=95) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
edge graphs16 and decision making17, deep learning has attracted 
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=105) width=251.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
increasing attention from researchers in other disciplines, including 
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=116) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
materials science18, chemistry19, laser physics20, particle physics21, 
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=126) width=251.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
quantum mechanics22, computational imaging23 and microscopy24, 
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=137) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
demonstrating  potential to circumvent the drawbacks  of  tradi-
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=148) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tional methods and create unprecedented opportunities in these 
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=158) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
areas. The unique advantages of deep learning lie in its data-driven 
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=169) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
methodology, which allows the model to discover useful informa-
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=179) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tion automatically from a huge amount of data, in sharp contrast 
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=190) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
to physics- or rule-based approaches. Over the past few years, deep 
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=200) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
learning has become a radically new approach in the context of pho-
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=211) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tonic design.
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=221) width=47.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
In this Review, we draw attention to a collection of recent results 
page=2 block=49361248 (x=14 y=232) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
that showcase the power of deep learning in the design of photonic 
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=242) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
structures, materials and devices, where empirical or traditional 
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=253) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
approaches  are infeasible or inefficient.  We begin by providing 
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=263) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
background on the deep-learning model for photonics and high-
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=274) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
lighting the formulation, development and advantages of deep neu-
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=284) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ral networks. Then we discuss several major model architectures, 
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=295) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
from the basic multilayer perceptron (MLP) and advanced deep 
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=305) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
neural networks to hybrid models with other optimization methods, 
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=316) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
emphasizing their potential to design photonic crystals, metama-
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=326) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
terials, plasmonic nanostructures and integrated silicon photonic 
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=337) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
devices (Fig. 1). The models can map design parameters (such as 
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=347) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
geometry, material, topology and spatial arrangement) and optical 
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=358) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
characteristics (such as polarization, phase, wavelength and orbital 
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=368) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
angular momentum), enabling both forward prediction and inverse 
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=379) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
design. Finally, we comment on the challenges and perspectives of 
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=389) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
this emerging interdisciplinary research area, with its potential to 
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=400) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
create a new science and engineering paradigm in which photonics 
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=410) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and artificial intelligence (AI) are interfaced with each other.
page=2 block=49361248 (x=3 y=421) width=224.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Background
page=2 block=9833487 (x=3 y=441) width=50.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Whitney-Semibold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=22
Historically, deep learning can be traced back to the 1940s, and it 
page=2 block=49470196 (x=3 y=452) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
went through many different names before becoming popularly 
page=2 block=49470196 (x=3 y=463) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
known by this term25. Originally, some of the learning algorithms 
page=2 block=49470196 (x=3 y=473) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
were intended to computationally model the process of biological 
page=2 block=49470196 (x=3 y=484) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
learning—that is, to model how learning happens in human brains. 
page=2 block=49470196 (x=3 y=494) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Therefore, deep learning was known by the term artificial neural 
page=2 block=49470196 (x=3 y=505) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
networks (ANNs) since the 1980s, accompanied by the second wave 
page=2 block=49470196 (x=3 y=515) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of AI research, which largely emerged through a movement called 
page=2 block=49470196 (x=3 y=526) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
connectionism26. During this period, the milestone was the famous 
page=2 block=49470196 (x=3 y=536) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
paper written by David Rumelhart, Geoffrey Hinton and Ronald 
page=2 block=49470196 (x=3 y=547) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Williams in 198627, in which the authors modified and reiterated 
page=2 block=49470196 (x=3 y=557) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the importance of the original back-propagation algorithm, mak-
page=2 block=49470196 (x=3 y=568) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ing it much faster than earlier approaches to learning and allowing 
page=2 block=49470196 (x=3 y=578) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
it to solve previously insoluble problems. The modern era of neu-
page=2 block=49470196 (x=3 y=589) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ral network research began with a breakthrough in 2006: Hinton, 
page=2 block=49470196 (x=3 y=599) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
who later coined the term deep learning, showed that deep neural 
page=2 block=49470196 (x=3 y=610) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
networks could be efficiently trained using a strategy called greedy 
page=2 block=49470196 (x=3 y=620) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
layer-wise pretraining28. Deep learning then gained broad popular-
page=2 block=49470196 (x=3 y=630) width=249.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ity, as researchers were able to train deeper neural networks than 
page=2 block=49470196 (x=3 y=641) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
had been possible before, and the importance of depth of the model 
page=2 block=49470196 (x=3 y=652) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
architecture was theoretically realized. Aided by the ever-increasing 
page=2 block=49470196 (x=3 y=662) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
scale of available data, deep learning still leads the current boom in 
page=2 block=49470196 (x=3 y=673) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
AI research, with the performance surpassing previous models by a 
page=2 block=49470196 (x=3 y=683) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
large margin, or even beating humans29.
page=2 block=49470196 (x=3 y=693) width=147.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
In the context of photonics research, the introduction of deep 
page=2 block=49470196 (x=14 y=704) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
learning (or more appropriately ANNs, at that time) dated back to 
page=2 block=49470196 (x=3 y=715) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the 1990s during the second prevalence period of AI research. This 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=32) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
interdisciplinary field primarily featured extensive work from the 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=43) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
microwave community, where ANNs were used as a computer-aided 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=53) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
design  tool  for  fast  prototyping  of  microwave  devices  and 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=64) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
radio-frequency circuits30. The success of ANN-aided design was 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=74) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
attributed to the revival of the MLP, the simplest form of a feedfor-
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=85) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ward neural network that comprises several fully connected layers. 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=95) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Each neuron in a layer is connected to all neurons in the next layer 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=106) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
with its own unique weight. Therefore, this is also called a fully con-
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=116) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
nected neural network or a dense neural network. At this early stage, 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=127) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the application of ANNs was straightforward. The design problem 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=137) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
was often transformed to training an ANN that linked an input port 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=148) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
with an output port. According to specific tasks, different variables, 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=158) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
such as circuit or device parameters (for example, geometry, physi-
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=169) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
cal property, bias or frequency) or performance parameters (for 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=179) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
example, S-parameters, voltage, current or power) were carefully 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=190) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
chosen to feed to each port. Based on this scheme, many microwave 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=200) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
design problems were readily solved by ANNs, including transmis-
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=211) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
sion lines31, vias32, filters33, amplifiers34 and antennas35.
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=221) width=199.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
The use of ANNs in microwave and other photonic designs fol-
page=2 block=47732256 (x=275 y=232) width=238.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
lows a standard supervised learning paradigm, which aims to find 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=242) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
a mapping between input variable X and output variable Y. The 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=253) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
training process is to optimize the model parameters to make it an 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=263) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
accurate representation of the desired mapping, on a previously col-
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=274) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
lected training dataset containing examples of X and its correspond-
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=284) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ing label Y. Starting with random initialization, this data-driven 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=295) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
learning approach modifies the model parameters iteratively on the 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=305) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
training dataset according to a specific loss function evaluated on 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=316) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
true labels Y, until a convergence occurs and the generalized model 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=326) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
is able to predict unseen data. It is very different from conventional 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=337) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
optimization approaches, either gradient-based or gradient-free, 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=347) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
in which no such pre-collected input–output pairs are available, 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=358) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and the optimization of given targets is guided by certain rules 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=368) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
in a case-by-case manner. To efficiently train an ANN model, the 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=379) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
back-propagation algorithm was developed. Detailed explanations 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=389) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of the basic ANN structure and the back-propagation algorithm are 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=400) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
presented in Box 1 and Box 2, respectively.
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=410) width=157.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
The interplay between microwave research and ANNs, as the 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=275 y=421) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
first attempt to combine electromagnetics with AI, was not a coin-
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=431) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
cidence. On the one hand, most designs of microwave devices can 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=442) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
be decomposed into optimizing some parameters for a given target, 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=452) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
both of which can be conveniently represented by a few variables. 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=463) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
With well-understood physics, mature simulation tools and thus lit-
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=473) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tle difficulty in data acquisition, finding the relations between these 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=484) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
variables is less complicated for an MLP model. On the other hand, 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=494) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
more complex photonic designs such as plasmonic nanostructures, 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=505) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
metamaterials, photonic crystals or silicon photonic devices were 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=515) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
still in their infancy, with limited understanding in the commu-
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=526) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
nity. These structures and devices, featuring deep sub-wavelength 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=536) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
dimensions, large design flexibility, extreme dispersions or compli-
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=547) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
cated performance characteristics, were not tractable to the shallow 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=557) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ANN model at that time. Related research then stagnated until the 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=568) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
recent epoch-making development of deep learning.
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=578) width=193.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
The past decade has witnessed the rise of deep learning with 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=275 y=589) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
unprecedented impact on a plethora of research topics. With the 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=599) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
invention of new training and regularization techniques such as 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=610) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
‘ReLU’  (rectified linear units)  activation36,  dropout37  and batch 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=620) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
normalization38, it is now feasible to design and train deeper and 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=630) width=251.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
deeper neural networks that can exploit larger datasets with better 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=641) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
performance. Meanwhile, advanced model architectures have been 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=652) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
proposed or improved to solve tasks in specific fields of machine 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=662) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
learning and pattern recognition, such as the convolutional neural 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=673) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
network (CNN)13 for image recognition, recurrent neural network 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=683) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
(RNN)39 for  natural  language processing,  generative adversarial 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=693) width=251.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
network (GAN)40 and variational autoencoder (VAE)41 for image 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=704) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
generation. The photonics community also benefited from the rapid 
page=2 block=47732256 (x=264 y=715) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

model architectures (for example, multilayer perceptron, convolutional network, recurrent network, generative model or hybrid model) with specific

into consideration both optical characteristics (such as polarization, phase, wavelength and orbital angular momentum) and design parameters (such as

page=3 block=18875072 (x=4 y=176) width=72.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=SWMXCJ+Helvetica wordint.min=1 wordint.max=3
Multilayer perceptron
page=3 block=15992814 (x=4 y=406) width=61.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=SWMXCJ+Helvetica wordint.min=1 wordint.max=3
Convolutional network
page=3 block=503318815 (x=120 y=467) width=64.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=SWMXCJ+Helvetica wordint.min=1 wordint.max=3
advances in deep-learning techniques. Unlike rule-guided optimi-
page=3 block=32966782 (x=2 y=610) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
zation that explores the design space by following certain strate-
page=3 block=32966782 (x=2 y=620) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
gies case by case, deep learning, as a data-driven method, aims to 
page=3 block=32966782 (x=2 y=631) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
describe the design space holistically, using the training data as 
page=3 block=32966782 (x=2 y=641) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
samples. Therefore, with generalization ability within a given design 
page=3 block=32966782 (x=2 y=652) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
space, deep learning can produce fast and accurate designs without 
page=3 block=32966782 (x=2 y=662) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the need for case-by-case, time-consuming numerical calculations. 
page=3 block=32966782 (x=2 y=673) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Well-trained deep-learning models can directly set up a mapping 
page=3 block=32966782 (x=2 y=683) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
from design to optical properties of target photonic devices, and 
page=3 block=32966782 (x=2 y=694) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
vice versa. In addition, deep learning can interact with traditional 
page=3 block=32966782 (x=2 y=704) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
optimization methods to improve the algorithm performance.
page=3 block=32966782 (x=2 y=715) width=229.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

In this section, we will discuss some relevant model architectures 
page=3 block=31271212 (x=263 y=620) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and their applications in solving photonics problems.
page=3 block=31271212 (x=263 y=631) width=196.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Multilayer perceptron. Although the architecture is rather sim-
page=3 block=31305660 (x=263 y=652) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ple, the MLP model, as illustrated in Fig. 1, has been theoretically 
page=3 block=31305660 (x=263 y=662) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
proven as a universal approximator that is capable of fitting any 
page=3 block=31305660 (x=263 y=673) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
continuous functions with a finite number of neurons42. In modern 
page=3 block=31305660 (x=263 y=683) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
deep-learning models with sophisticated and task-specific architec-
page=3 block=31305660 (x=263 y=694) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tures, MLP often serves as a bottleneck layer to extract meaningful 
page=3 block=31305660 (x=263 y=704) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
features as a compact representation of high-dimensional data such

Box 1 | Fully connected neural network
page=4 block=76021970 (x=8 y=35) width=145.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Whitney-Semibold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=3
A fully connected neural network with L layers consists of one 
page=4 block=125836196 (x=8 y=58) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
input layer, one output layer and L ? 2 hidden layers. Each neu-
page=4 block=125836196 (x=8 y=69) width=238.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ron in a given layer is connected to every neuron in the next 
page=4 block=125836196 (x=8 y=79) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
layer, but without interlayer connections. The neurons process 
page=4 block=125836196 (x=8 y=90) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the input data layer by layer with learnable parameters, namely 
page=4 block=125836196 (x=8 y=100) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
weights and biases. Specifically, let us denote the weight from the 
page=4 block=125836196 (x=8 y=111) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
kth neuron in the (m ? 1)th layer to the jth neuron in the mth 
page=4 block=125836196 (x=8 y=121) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
layer as wmjk, and the bias of the jth neuron in the mth layer as bmj . 
page=4 block=125836196 (x=8 y=131) width=240.000000 height=9.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Then, theI output of the jth neuron in the mth layer, denoted Ias 
page=4 block=125836196 (x=8 y=142) width=240.000000 height=8.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
amj , can be computed as a weighted summation of the outputs 
page=4 block=125836196 (x=9 y=152) width=239.000000 height=9.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
frIom the previous layer zmj  followed by a nonlinear activation 
page=4 block=125836196 (x=8 y=162) width=240.000000 height=10.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
function ?(·), that is
page=4 block=125836196 (x=8 y=174) width=74.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
I
page=4 block=20644720 (x=105 y=176) width=3.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular2 wordint.min=17 wordint.max=17
?X

In a matrix and vector notation, the learna e mdel parameters 
page=4 block=125881497 (x=20 y=221) width=228.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
iann dla yae rb imas  ivnecclutodre  bam w¼eighbtmj ja1t?rijx? jwjjI. mTh¼e nwomjknlijn1e?ajr? jfjju;1n?ckt?iojknj  
page=4 block=125881497 (x=8 y=231) width=240.000000 height=21.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
?(·), also known as anI activation function, is crucial as it enables 
page=4 block=125881497 (x=8 y=253) width=240.000000 height=9.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the neural network to tackle highly complex data representation 
page=4 block=125881497 (x=8 y=263) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
instead of degrading to a simple linear mapping. Popular choices 
page=4 block=125881497 (x=8 y=274) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of activation function include the sigmoid, hyperbolic tangents 
page=4 block=125881497 (x=8 y=284) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
or rectified linear units. Consequently, in the forward pass, the 
page=4 block=125881497 (x=8 y=295) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
input data flow from one layer to the next, being linearly summed 
page=4 block=125881497 (x=8 y=305) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and nonlinearly activated at each stage, similar to the electrical 
page=4 block=125881497 (x=8 y=316) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
signal flowing in biological neurons and synapses (shown as 
page=4 block=125881497 (x=8 y=326) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
black arrows).
page=4 block=125881497 (x=8 y=337) width=52.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
To estimate the performance of the neural network, a cost 
page=4 block=125881497 (x=20 y=347) width=228.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
function C(·) should be defined to measure the  discrepancy 
page=4 block=125881497 (x=8 y=358) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
between the network output and the desired output. The cost 
page=4 block=125881497 (x=8 y=368) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
function takes the outputs of the last layer and the ground-truth 
page=4 block=125881497 (x=8 y=379) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
label as input and returns  a scalar value as the error to be 
page=4 block=125881497 (x=8 y=389) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
minimized.  To  adjust  the  model  parameters  properly,  the 
page=4 block=125881497 (x=8 y=400) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
learning algorithm computes the gradient of the cost function 
page=4 block=125881497 (x=8 y=410) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
with respect to each weight and bias, indicating how much the 
page=4 block=125881497 (x=8 y=421) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
error would increase or decrease if the parameter is increased by 
page=4 block=125881497 (x=8 y=431) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
a tiny amount. Then each parameter is adjusted in the opposite 
page=4 block=125881497 (x=8 y=442) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
direction to its gradient.
page=4 block=125881497 (x=8 y=452) width=90.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
m
page=4 block=36441800 (x=139 y=473) width=4.000000 height=3.000000 fontname=CYKFMP+Helvetica-Oblique wordint.min=11 wordint.max=11

page=4 block=341318640 (x=186 y=603) width=28.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=SWMXCJ+Helvetica wordint.min=1 wordint.max=3
as images. Even before the astounding success of deep learning in 
page=4 block=49387666 (x=3 y=662) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
computer vision13, researchers explored MLP in photonics-related 
page=4 block=49387666 (x=3 y=672) width=251.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
domains following the design schemes devised by the microwave 
page=4 block=49387666 (x=3 y=683) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
community. The potency of these models was largely limited by 
page=4 block=49387666 (x=3 y=694) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
immature training strategy, lack of data and thus shallow model 
page=4 block=49387666 (x=3 y=704) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
architecture.
page=4 block=49387666 (x=3 y=715) width=46.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=1 wordint.max=21
In 2018, Itzik Malkiel and co-workers reported a bidirectional 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=275 y=32) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
MLP-based deep-learning model that could be used to design plas-
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=43) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
monic nanostructures43. In an advance on earlier work, the research-
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=53) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ers managed to model the intricate physical relationship between 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=64) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
photonic  structures  and  their  optical  characteristics  by  resort-
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=74) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ing to a ‘deeper’ network configuration. As shown in Fig. 2a, the 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=85) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
structure of interest is an H-shaped metallic structure represented 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=95) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
by eight parameters: three continuous parameters (the length and 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=106) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
rotation angle of arms) and five binary parameters (existence of 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=116) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
certain arms). The design targets are two reflection spectra under 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=127) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the illumination of light polarized along the horizontal or vertical 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=137) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
direction. Each spectrum is discretized into 43 data points, and the 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=148) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
material properties (such as permittivity of the indium tin oxide 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=158) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
adhesion layer and hosting materials) are represented as a vector of 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=169) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
25 parameters. A geometry-predicting network of eight group lay-
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=179) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ers and a spectrum-predicting network of six layers are trained on 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=190) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
18,000 samples so that the model can simultaneously function as 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=200) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
a fast simulator and an inverse design tool. The scale of the data-
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=211) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
set, input complexity and the depth of the model show that, from 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=221) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
a data-driven view, deeper models armed with larger datasets are a 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=232) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
potential alternative approach to solve photonic design tasks.
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=242) width=225.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Meanwhile, to deal with specific photonic problems, adapta-
page=4 block=47732256 (x=275 y=253) width=238.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tion of the MLP-based model burgeoned, with improvements in 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=263) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the model architecture, training strategy and application. Wei Ma 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=274) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and co-workers reported a deep-learning model with two bidi-
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=284) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
rectional neural networks that were integrated by an ensembled 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=295) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
learning strategy  to  achieve  on-demand  design of chiral meta-
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=305) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
materials, which exhibit distinct responses when incident light is 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=316) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
left-circularly polarized (LCP) or right-circularly polarized (RCP)44. 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=326) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
The chiral metamaterial comprises two twisted split-ring resona-
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=337) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tors placed on a metallic back-reflector (left panel in Fig. 2b). Two 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=347) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
bidirectional neural networks, termed the primary network and 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=358) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
auxiliary network respectively, are constructed to model the inter-
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=368) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
connection of three physical quantities, namely reflection spectra, 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=379) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
circular dichroism spectra and design parameters. By introducing 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=389) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the auxiliary network, the accuracy of the forward prediction of 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=400) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
spectra around resonances (middle panel in Fig. 2b), as well as the 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=410) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
inverse retrieval of the design parameters, is substantially improved. 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=421) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
The model functionality is also extended, enabling retrieval of pos-
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=431) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
sible metamaterial  design from simple requirements on several 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=442) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
parameters of desired chiroptical  response, or direct prediction 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=452) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of the circular dichroism spectrum from design parameters (right 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=463) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
panel in Fig. 2b).
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=473) width=62.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
When trained on large datasets, MLP-based models for inverse 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=275 y=484) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
design often fail to converge, since there exist multiple candidates 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=494) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
satisfying similar requirements but with very different designs. To 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=505) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
solve this, Dianjing Liu and colleagues proposed a tandem train-
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=515) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ing method for inverse design45. The idea is to first train a forward 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=525) width=251.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
modelling network, mapping the design to optical responses. This 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=536) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
pretrained forward network is then connected to the output of the 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=547) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
inverse design network, with the forward prediction error serving as 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=557) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the supervision signal. By indirectly training in this tandem config-
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=568) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
uration, the inverse retrieval outputs are forced to converge to only 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=578) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
one possibility guided by the forward model, efficiently solving the 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=589) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
data inconsistency issue that arises from the fundamental property 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=599) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of non-uniqueness in the inverse design problem.
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=610) width=183.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Instead of focusing solely on the inputs and outputs, MLP-based 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=275 y=620) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
models, as universal approximators of functions, also allow gradi-
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=631) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ents to be calculated analytically. John Peurifoy and colleagues used 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=641) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
a single neural network to approximate light scattering by multi-
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=652) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
layer nanoparticles, where the analytical gradient obtained from the 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=662) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
model ewas used for structural optimization given specific require-
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=673) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ments in the spectra46. They showed that, guided by the analytical 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=683) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
gradient from an MLP model, the single-band high scattering effect 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=694) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
in core–shell nanoparticles can be efficiently optimized (left panel 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=704) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
in Fig. 2c). They also made a quantitative comparison between a 
page=4 block=47732256 (x=264 y=715) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

With the partial derivatives of the cost function C with respect 
page=5 block=110169103 (x=19 y=421) width=228.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
to all of the learnable parameters in the neural network — that 
page=5 block=110169103 (x=7 y=431) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
is, the weight matrices wl and bias vectors bl for all layers (l = 1, 
page=5 block=110169103 (x=7 y=441) width=240.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
2, …, L) — the model can then be trained by stochastic gradient 
page=5 block=110169103 (x=7 y=452) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
descent (SGD). In the SGD  algorithm, the training data are 
page=5 block=110169103 (x=7 y=463) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
shuffled and then divided into several batches, each containing 
page=5 block=110169103 (x=7 y=473) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
only a small portion of all data. The training step consists of 
page=5 block=110169103 (x=7 y=484) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
feeding the network with a batch of data, computing the outputs 
page=5 block=110169103 (x=7 y=494) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and the errors, back-propagating to compute the average gradient 
page=5 block=110169103 (x=7 y=505) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
for this batch, and adjusting the weights and biases accordingly.
page=5 block=110169103 (x=7 y=515) width=235.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Concretely, we need to define a hyper-parameter called the 
page=5 block=110169103 (x=19 y=526) width=228.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
learning rate ?, to control how much the parameters are modified 
page=5 block=110169103 (x=7 y=536) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
as a portion of their gradients with respect to the cost in this 
page=5 block=110169103 (x=7 y=547) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
batch. For a batch containing M training data,  according to 
page=5 block=110169103 (x=7 y=557) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
equations (4) and (5), we have the following update rules for 
page=5 block=110169103 (x=7 y=568) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
weights and biases:
page=5 block=110169103 (x=7 y=578) width=71.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
wl ! wl ? ? X
