The training step is repeated many times until the value of 
page=5 block=110132256 (x=19 y=662) width=228.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the cost stops decreasing. After training, the performance of the 
page=5 block=110132256 (x=7 y=673) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
model is evaluated on a different test set, containing samples 
page=5 block=110132256 (x=7 y=683) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
not seen by the model during training, to test its generalization 
page=5 block=110132256 (x=7 y=694) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ability.
page=5 block=110132256 (x=7 y=704) width=25.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=1 wordint.max=21
deep  neural  network  and  interior-point  methods,  a  numerical 
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=32) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
inverse design algorithm suitable for nanoparticles. Under the same 
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=43) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
error threshold during inverse design, the runtime of the neural 
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=53) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
network is two orders of magnitude shorter than numerical meth-
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=64) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ods. Even more notably, as illustrated in the right panel of Fig. 2c, 
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=74) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
with the increase of design complexity (that is, number of nanopar-
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=85) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ticle layers), the neural network shows a linear increase in runtime, 
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=95) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
in contrast to a polynomial increase in runtime (with an exponent 
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=106) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of 4.5) for numerical methods. These results indicate that within 
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=116) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the same accuracy standard, neural networks, once fully trained, 
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=127) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
beat conventional numerical inverse design methods by a large mar-
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=137) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
gin in speed.
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=148) width=47.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Following these first forays into applying data-driven method-
page=5 block=31273312 (x=274 y=158) width=238.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ology in photonics research, deep MLP models and their variants 
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=169) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
have been  extensively  studied  for many  photonic design tasks, 
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=179) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
including topological photonics47,  integrated silicon  photonics48, 
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=189) width=251.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
colour generation from nanostructures49,50, metamaterials51–53, plas-
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=200) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
monic structures54 and photonic crystals55. For instance, Fig. 2d 
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=210) width=251.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
illustrates topological  photonic structures whose edge-state  dis-
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=221) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
persion is well reconstructed by an MLP model47. In work shown 
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=231) width=251.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
in Fig. 2e, the researchers propose to use deep learning to design 
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=242) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
silicon-on-insulator-based 1 × 2 power splitters with various target 
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=253) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
transmission ratios at two ports (T1 and T2), in which the device 
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=263) width=251.000000 height=8.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
structure is represented by a matrix of binary variables, indicat-
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=274) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ing whether the silicon is etched or not at certain coordinates48. In 
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=284) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Fig. 2f, the MLP approach is applied in colour-generation meta-
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=295) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
surfaces, and colour pixels composed of HfO2 nanopillars are opti-
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=305) width=249.000000 height=8.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
mized by the model to form a wide colour gamut49.
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=315) width=188.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
In addition to design problems, researchers have also developed 
page=5 block=31273312 (x=274 y=326) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
sophisticated MLP models to tackle other problems in photonics, 
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=337) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
such as modal classification and effective refractive index retrieval 
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=347) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
in waveguides56,57, dimension reduction to reveal underlying phys-
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=357) width=249.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ics58 and near-field manipulation of plasmonic nanoantennas59. As 
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=368) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the plain form of the deep-learning model, MLP requires vectorized 
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=379) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
input and output. By parameterizing model inputs and outputs into 
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=389) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
vectors composed of several discrete and dimensionless elements, 
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=400) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the MLP model can be adapted to a broad range of photonic appli-
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=410) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
cations regardless of the underlying physics.
page=5 block=31273312 (x=263 y=421) width=163.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Advanced deep-learning techniques. Although MLP models offer 
page=5 block=31263324 (x=263 y=442) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
an effective approach for many photonic tasks, the simple connec-
page=5 block=31310844 (x=263 y=452) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tions in the model still pose some difficulties when the intrinsic 
page=5 block=31310844 (x=263 y=463) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
structure, target response and design space are either multimodal 
page=5 block=31310844 (x=263 y=473) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
or  hard  to  parameterize.  Therefore,  application-specific  model 
page=5 block=31310844 (x=263 y=484) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
architectures have been invented for photonic research or trans-
page=5 block=31310844 (x=263 y=494) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
planted from other fields, including CNNs specialized to input 
page=5 block=31310844 (x=263 y=505) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
with local semantic correlation, RNNs for time-dependent inputs 
page=5 block=31310844 (x=263 y=515) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and deep generative models to deal with structured-output con-
page=5 block=31310844 (x=263 y=526) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ditions. Such advanced models can extend the functionality and 
page=5 block=31310844 (x=263 y=536) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
improve performance in many photonic design problems, which 
page=5 block=31310844 (x=263 y=547) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
are discussed below.
page=5 block=31310844 (x=263 y=557) width=75.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Convolutional neural networks. In deep learning, a  CNN is a 
page=5 block=31343326 (x=263 y=578) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
class of deep networks that extracts the features of the inputs by 
page=5 block=31343326 (x=263 y=589) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
using convolution operations on the output of each layer. Owing 
page=5 block=31343326 (x=263 y=599) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
to the nature of the convolution operation, a CNN can capture the 
page=5 block=31343326 (x=263 y=610) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
local  correlation of spatial information in  images. In photonics 
page=5 block=31343326 (x=263 y=620) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
research, a CNN is an ideal candidate to process data represented in 
page=5 block=31343326 (x=263 y=631) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
high-dimensional spaces, such as photonic patterns represented as 
page=5 block=31343326 (x=263 y=641) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
images, and spectral responses of given photonic devices. As a coun-
page=5 block=31343326 (x=263 y=652) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
terpart of CNN, transposed CNN (TCNN) uses the convolution in 
page=5 block=31343326 (x=263 y=662) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
‘reversed’ order, enabling the generation of high-dimensional data 
page=5 block=31343326 (x=263 y=673) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
from low-dimensional vectors. TCNNs are commonly implemented 
page=5 block=31343326 (x=263 y=683) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
as a part of generative models for the discovery and design of pho-
page=5 block=31343326 (x=263 y=694) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tonic structures with large degrees of freedom. The basic architec-
page=5 block=31343326 (x=263 y=704) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tures of CNN- and TCNN-based generative models that transform 
page=5 block=31343326 (x=263 y=715) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
81
page=5 block=231673445 (x=505 y=737) width=7.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=Whitney-Bold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=16

page=6 block=14028379 (x=250 y=735) width=263.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=Whitney-Book wordint.min=1 wordint.max=3
82
page=6 block=1576549 (x=3 y=737) width=8.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=Whitney-Bold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=16
data between high-dimensional images and low-dimensional fea-
page=6 block=49356192 (x=3 y=32) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ture vectors are illustrated in the lower panels of Fig. 1 (second and 
page=6 block=49356192 (x=3 y=43) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
fourth insets, respectively).
page=6 block=49356192 (x=3 y=53) width=100.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
CNNs have been used in various optics and photonics prob-
page=6 block=49356192 (x=14 y=64) width=238.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
lems, such as the inverse scattering problem60, wavefront correc-
page=6 block=49356192 (x=3 y=74) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tion61, digital coding metasurfaces62,63, and prediction of optical 
page=6 block=49356192 (x=3 y=84) width=251.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
properties in complex photonic and materials systems64,65. The 
page=6 block=49356192 (x=3 y=95) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
convolution  operation  in a  CNN is translationally symmetric, 
page=6 block=49356192 (x=3 y=106) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
making it suitable to model periodic photonic structures such 
page=6 block=49356192 (x=3 y=116) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
as photonic crystals or metamaterials. These structures, usually 
page=6 block=49356192 (x=3 y=127) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
represented by two-dimensional (2D) images, inherently satisfy 
page=6 block=49356192 (x=3 y=137) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
translation invariance when the periodic boundary condition is 
page=6 block=49356192 (x=3 y=148) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
applied in the numerical simulations. Recently, Takashi Asano and 
page=6 block=49356192 (x=3 y=158) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
co-workers used a neural network consisting of CNNs to approxi-
page=6 block=49356192 (x=3 y=169) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
mate the Q-factor of photonic crystals55. Back-propagation was 
page=6 block=49356192 (x=3 y=179) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
used to optimize the positions of nanocavities to greatly improve 
page=6 block=49356192 (x=3 y=190) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the Q-factor from 3.8 × 108 to 1.6 × 109, as shown in Fig. 3a. 
page=6 block=49356192 (x=3 y=200) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Besides the basic architectures of CNNs, advanced network struc-
page=6 block=49356192 (x=3 y=211) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tures including residual networks66 and inception networks67 have 
page=6 block=49356192 (x=3 y=221) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
been introduced to enhance the performance and capability of the 
page=6 block=49356192 (x=3 y=232) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
network. These network structures can be used to train surrogate 
page=6 block=49356192 (x=3 y=242) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
models for the simulation of complex photonic structures with 
page=6 block=49356192 (x=3 y=253) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
large degrees of freedom68.
page=6 block=49356192 (x=3 y=263) width=100.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Recurrent neural networks. RNNs tackle problems associated with 
page=6 block=49407112 (x=3 y=284) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
sequential data such as sentences and audio signals. As shown in 
page=6 block=49407112 (x=3 y=295) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Fig. 1 (lower panels, third inset), the network receives sequential 
page=6 block=49407112 (x=3 y=305) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
data one at a time and incrementally generates new data series. For 
page=6 block=49407112 (x=3 y=316) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
photonic design, RNNs are suitable to model optical signals or spec-
page=6 block=49407112 (x=3 y=326) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tra in the time domain with a specific line shape originated from 
page=6 block=49407112 (x=3 y=337) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
various modes of the resonance. RNNs have been implemented 
page=6 block=49407112 (x=3 y=347) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
to analyse optical signals and equalize noises in high-speed fibre 
page=6 block=49407112 (x=3 y=358) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
transmission69. In combination with CNNs, RNNs were also used 
page=6 block=49407112 (x=3 y=368) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
to improve the approximation of the optical responses of nano-
page=6 block=49407112 (x=3 y=379) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
structures represented in images68. Figure 3b (left panel) presents 
page=6 block=49407112 (x=3 y=389) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
random silver nanostructures to be simulated. With the help of an 
page=6 block=49407112 (x=3 y=400) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
RNN, the network is able to predict the absorption of the structure 
page=6 block=49407112 (x=3 y=410) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
from 800 nm to 1,700 nm, showing excellent agreement with full 
page=6 block=49407112 (x=3 y=421) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
wave simulation (Fig. 3b, right panel). The performance of RNNs 
page=6 block=49407112 (x=3 y=431) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
can be enhanced by adopting advanced varieties of RNNs, such as 
page=6 block=49407112 (x=3 y=442) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
long short-term memory39 and gated recurrent units14. Network 
page=6 block=49407112 (x=3 y=452) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
systems hybridizing CNNs and RNNs are promising techniques to 
page=6 block=49407112 (x=3 y=463) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
model and design photonic devices that manipulate unconventional 
page=6 block=49407112 (x=3 y=473) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
spatiotemporal properties of light.
page=6 block=49407112 (x=3 y=484) width=126.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Deep generative models. Instead of determining the conditional 
page=6 block=49377898 (x=3 y=505) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
distribution  and  thus  decision  boundaries,  generative  models 
page=6 block=49377898 (x=3 y=515) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
describe the joint distribution of the input and output to  opti-
page=6 block=49377898 (x=3 y=526) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
mize a certain objective in a probabilistically generative manner. 
page=6 block=49377898 (x=3 y=536) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Unlike their deterministic counterparts, such models can handle 
page=6 block=49377898 (x=3 y=547) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
one-to-many  mapping  and  produce  structured  outputs  given 
page=6 block=49377898 (x=3 y=557) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
prescribed  requirements,  as  illustrated in Fig.  1 (lower  panels, 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=32) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
fourth inset). Empowered by deep-learning algorithms, deep gen-
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=43) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
erative models can produce data that replicate in the same way as 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=53) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
or are similar to the training dataset. An autoencoder (AE) consists 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=64) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of an encoder and a decoder. The encoder maps the training data 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=74) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
into a reduced-dimensional space, that is, latent space, while the 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=85) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
decoder reconstructs the variable in the latent space into the train-
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=95) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ing data. AEs are used to reduce the dimensionality of the design 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=106) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
space and optical response features of photonic devices, providing 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=116) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
essential information on the light–matter interaction for device 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=127) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
optimization58.
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=137) width=55.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Similar to an  AE, a  variational autoencoder  (VAE) is con-
page=6 block=47727200 (x=275 y=148) width=238.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
structed by adding probabilistic perturbation in the latent space 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=158) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of the AE41. The structure of a VAE is illustrated in Fig. 3c (top 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=168) width=251.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
panel). Because the latent space is a continuous representation of 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=179) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the training data, new designs can be constructed by sampling the 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=190) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
latent space70. Leveraging the reduced dimension and continu-
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=200) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ity of the latent space, Wei Ma and colleagues used a VAE-based 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=211) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
generative model to encode the meta-atoms of double-layered 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=221) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
chiral metamaterials and their  optical responses,  enabling  the 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=232) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
investigation of the complex relationship between structure and 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=242) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
performance without extensive data collection71. Figure 3d (left 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=252) width=251.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
panel) shows the required circular dichroism spectrum with a 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=263) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
sharp peak at 60 THz. The VAE-based framework can identify a 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=274) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
double-layered metamaterial with a circular dichroism spectrum 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=284) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
replicating the desired one (right panel). More importantly, the 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=295) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
model can solve the one-to-many mapping inverse problem and 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=305) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
come up with distinct metamaterial structures to satisfy the same 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=316) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
requirement of chiroptical responses.
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=326) width=141.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
The GAN, as shown in Fig. 3c (bottom panel), is another class of 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=275 y=337) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
deep generative model constructed with a generator and a discrimi-
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=347) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
nator40. The generator is trained in an adversarial manner to create 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=357) width=251.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
samples that, ideally, form a distribution indistinguishable from the 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=368) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
training dataset. With the ability to generate massive nanostruc-
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=379) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tures within short time, GANs have been used in the design and 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=389) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
optimization of dielectric and metallic metasurfaces in a stochastic 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=400) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
manner72. Recently, Zhaocheng Liu and co-workers have proposed 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=410) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
a framework  leveraging  GANs  to inversely  design  metasurface 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=421) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
nanostructures that match on-demand design objectives73. A GAN 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=431) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and a pretrained simulator jointly identify the topology of nano-
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=442) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
structures from a user-defined geometric dataset. In Fig. 3e, given 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=452) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
a user-defined transmittance spectrum for the inverse design prob-
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=463) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
lem (left panel), the framework can identify a nanostructure with 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=473) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
a spectral behaviour that matches the input one with high fidelity 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=484) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
(right panel).
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=494) width=49.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Besides the standard architecture of the GAN and VAE, some 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=275 y=505) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
other varieties of generative models can also be implemented for 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=515) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the discovery and design of photonic structures. For instance, 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=526) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
compositional pattern-producing networks (CPPNs) have been 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=536) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
reported as serving as a generator in the GAN framework to pro-
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=547) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
duce high-quality nanostructure patterns for the inverse design of 
page=6 block=47727200 (x=264 y=557) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

page=9 block=31359832 (x=263 y=652) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
a cluster of high-efficiency devices76. Physics-based gradients, cal-
page=9 block=31359832 (x=263 y=662) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
culated based on forward and adjoint electromagnetic simulations, 
page=9 block=31359832 (x=263 y=673) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
are used for error back-propagation during the training phase of 
page=9 block=31359832 (x=263 y=683) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the generator, ensuring the direct connection of the compressed 
page=9 block=31359832 (x=263 y=694) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
space formation of the generative network with enhancing device 
page=9 block=31359832 (x=263 y=704) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
efficiency.  Moreover,  such  an  approach  avoids  the  process  of

page=10 block=14028379 (x=250 y=735) width=263.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=Whitney-Book wordint.min=1 wordint.max=3

page=10 block=1576549 (x=3 y=737) width=8.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=Whitney-Bold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=16
generating the training set, since the generative network learns the 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=32) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
physical relationship between device geometry and optical response 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=43) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
directly through electromagnetic simulations. The loss function is 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=53) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
constructed such that it gives higher weight to devices with better 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=64) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
efficiency and decreases the impact of low-efficiency designs that 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=74) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
are potentially locked in local optima. As a result, one can nar-
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=85) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
row down the compressed space representation of the generative 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=95) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
network to the sub-domain of highly efficient designs. The pro-
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=106) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
posed approach only needs 10% of the computational cost of direct 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=116) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
adjoint-based topology optimization calculations.
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=127) width=184.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
More recently, an adversarial autoencoder (AAE), consisting of 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=14 y=137) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
three neural networks (encoder, decoder and discriminator), has 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=148) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
been coupled with a topology optimization framework for the devel-
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=158) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
opment of a thermal emitter design82. The AAE-based approach 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=168) width=251.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ensures almost ideal (98%) thermal emission reshaping efficiency 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=179) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and requires a third of the computation time of direct topology opti-
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=190) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
mization. By interfacing an AAE-based approach with a pre-trained 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=200) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
CNN network, one can achieve up to a 4,900-fold speed-up in com-
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=211) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
parison with direct topology optimization. Along with efficient 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=221) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
optimization search, such an approach promises unparalleled con-
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=232) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
trol over the data distribution within the compressed design space. 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=242) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
The latter fact opens up the possibility of performing global opti-
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=253) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
mization searches directly within the high-dimensional compressed 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=263) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
design space, avoiding time-consuming post-processing of the gen-
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=274) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
erated designs.
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=284) width=54.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Optimization  within  high-dimensional parametric  space  can 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=14 y=295) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
be efficiently realized by adapting one of the dimension-reduction 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=305) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
machine-learning algorithms. Recently, linear principal component 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=316) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
analysis has been used to map and characterize a multiparameter 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=326) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
design space of a photonic system83. As shown in Fig. 4d, the devel-
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=336) width=249.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
oped approach consists of three main steps: (1) construction of a 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=347) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
sparse collection of pre-optimized designs by applying conventional 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=358) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
optimization methods; (2) determination of a lower-dimensional 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=368) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
sub-space of highly efficient designs by applying a dimensionality 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=379) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
reduction algorithm to the high-dimensional parametric space of 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=389) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
pre-optimized designs; and (3) exhaustive mapping of the design 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=400) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
sub-space to determine the continuous region of highly efficient 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=410) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
designs. This approach has been applied for the multiparametric 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=421) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
optimization of a vertical fibre grating coupler (Fig. 4e). It has been 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=431) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
shown that by applying the dimensionality algorithm, the complex-
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=442) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ity of the problem can be exponentially scaled down and that the 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=452) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
continuous sub-space of grating couplers with comparable fibre 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=463) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
coupling efficiencies can be mapped84. Such global analysis ensures 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=473) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
efficient multiparametric optimization and reveals the limitations 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=484) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of performance or structure of the given design configuration.
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=494) width=230.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Researchers have developed another approach to address one of 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=14 y=505) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the major bottlenecks of the conventional optimization techniques, 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=515) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
namely intensive direct electromagnetic simulations. This approach 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=526) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
uses  data-driven  methods  for  accelerating  Maxwell’s  equation 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=536) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
solver, which is involved in the optimization process. The feasibility 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=547) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of using data-driven methods for solving partial differential equa-
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=557) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tions has been investigated very recently, with the demonstration 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=568) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of learning an ‘optimal’ approximation of derivatives needed for 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=578) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
time-domain simulations85 or using neural networks for solving 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=588) width=251.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
partial differential equations86,87. Particularly, it has been demon-
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=599) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
strated that data-driven methods allow nonlinear partial differential 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=610) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
equations to be solved in the time domain at resolutions 4 to 8 times 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=620) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
coarser than required by standard finite-difference methods. By 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=631) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
using the CNN, the generalized minimal residual algorithm for the 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=641) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
solution of frequency-domain Maxwell’s equations has been accel-
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=652) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
erated88. This approach realizes an order-of-magnitude reduction 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=662) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
in the number of iterations required for solving frequency-domain 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=673) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
wave equations when trained on a dataset of wavelength-splitting 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=683) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
gratings. The proposed approach can be directly applied within 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=694) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
gradient-descent-based optimization of a photonic device, where 
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=704) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
many simulations with similar material distribution are required.
page=10 block=49370656 (x=3 y=715) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Discussions and perspectives
page=10 block=2145386688 (x=264 y=32) width=124.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Whitney-Semibold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
In this section, we will comment on the directions, challenges and 
page=10 block=47710896 (x=264 y=43) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
perspectives of interfacing deep learning with photonics.
page=10 block=47710896 (x=264 y=53) width=209.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Deep learning for complete control of light. It is believed that 
page=10 block=47710652 (x=264 y=74) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
deep learning can serve as a powerful tool in finding complex rela-
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=85) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tions between a structure and its optical responses. Nevertheless, 
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=95) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
at present, most literature related to the topic of the present review 
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=106) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
is limited to the study of the optical properties of a structure in 
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=116) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
terms of the optical spectra (reflection, transmission, scattering, 
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=127) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
absorption and so on) under the illustration of linear or circular 
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=137) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
polarization. Spectra can be conveniently discretized into vectors43 
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=147) width=251.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
(single spectrum) or matrices44 (multiple spectra), and thus can be 
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=158) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
readily incorporated into the deep-learning model. To aid in the 
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=169) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
complete control of light, one immediate task that we need to work 
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=179) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
on is to enhance the capability of deep-learning models with more 
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=190) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
degrees of freedom, such as the phase74, angular momentum, trajec-
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=200) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tory, nonlinearity, topology and near-field distributions75. This will 
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=210) width=251.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
lead to new devices including but not limited to multidimensional 
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=221) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
meta-holograms showing distinct images at different wavelengths, 
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=232) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
topological  photonic  crystals for  robust  light  transport  that  is 
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=242) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
immune to defects, and controllable nanoscale hotspots to enhance 
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=253) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the emission of single quantum emitters.
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=263) width=150.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Unlike a direct discretization, some pre-processing steps or mod-
page=10 block=47739278 (x=275 y=274) width=238.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ification of the model input–output may be required accordingly. 
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=284) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
For instance, one could consider using a periodic output activation 
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=295) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
for phase retrieval or using dimension reduction83 when dealing 
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=305) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
with near-field characteristics. Transfer-learning techniques89 may 
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=315) width=251.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
also help to construct versatile models for complete light control 
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=326) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
from base models trained with spectral responses. Reinforcement 
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=337) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
learning is another  approach worthy  of  further exploration, to 
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=347) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
expand the photonic design capability50. Unlike supervised learning 
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=357) width=251.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
or unsupervised learning, reinforcement learning manages to max-
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=368) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
imize a cumulative reward by taking actions in the current envi-
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=379) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ronment. This indirect optimization process balances the current 
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=389) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
knowledge and unknown domains, and could potentially investi-
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=400) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
gate more optical properties of photonic design tasks to realize com-
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=410) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
plete control of light.
page=10 block=47739278 (x=264 y=421) width=77.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
The burden of data collection. Since deep neural networks contain 
page=10 block=47833526 (x=264 y=442) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
thousands to millions of learnable parameters, a gigantic amount of 
page=10 block=47833526 (x=264 y=452) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
labelled data is inevitable to train the network. However, generating 
page=10 block=47833526 (x=264 y=463) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
data requires physical simulations or experimental measurements. 
page=10 block=47833526 (x=264 y=473) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Collecting a massive dataset is not always practical. In these circum-
page=10 block=47833526 (x=264 y=484) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
stances, unsupervised and semi-supervised learning strategies can 
page=10 block=47833526 (x=264 y=494) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
be leveraged to alleviate the burden of data collection. Unlike super-
page=10 block=47833526 (x=264 y=505) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
vised learning, unsupervised learning algorithms, such as principal 
page=10 block=47833526 (x=264 y=515) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
component analysis, and AEs/VAEs/GANs, require only a small 
page=10 block=47833526 (x=264 y=526) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
number of labels, usually serving as tools for data clustering and 
page=10 block=47833526 (x=264 y=536) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
dimensionality reduction. The processed data from unsupervised 
page=10 block=47833526 (x=264 y=547) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
learning strategies can be analysed to unveil the structural param-
page=10 block=47833526 (x=264 y=557) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
eters of the photonic devices that have most impact, assisting the 
page=10 block=47833526 (x=264 y=568) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
neural network to recognize the essential information of the design 
page=10 block=47833526 (x=264 y=578) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
without redundant data collection90,91.
page=10 block=47833526 (x=264 y=588) width=140.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Massive data collection can also be mitigated by merging the 
page=10 block=47833526 (x=275 y=599) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
deep-learning  models  with  underlying  physics.  For  example, 
page=10 block=47833526 (x=264 y=610) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
instead of training a model with enormous datasets to approximate 
page=10 block=47833526 (x=264 y=620) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the bidirectional relationship between physical structures and their 
page=10 block=47833526 (x=264 y=631) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
responses, deep learning can be adopted as intermediate steps to 
page=10 block=47833526 (x=264 y=641) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
efficiently  solve  partial  differential equations that  describe  the 
page=10 block=47833526 (x=264 y=652) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
physical systems87,88,92. Alternatively, learning the governing laws 
page=10 block=47833526 (x=264 y=662) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
behind the physical phenomena can also reduce the dependence on 
page=10 block=47833526 (x=264 y=673) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
data86,93. Deep learning in this strategy is usually used to extract fea-
page=10 block=47833526 (x=264 y=683) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tures from limited data for the regression of a few parameters that 
page=10 block=47833526 (x=264 y=694) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
parametrizes the models or equations of the system. Furthermore, 
page=10 block=47833526 (x=264 y=704) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
it is possible to generate a solution that satisfies certain partial 
page=10 block=47833526 (x=264 y=715) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

NAture PHotoNIcs

differential equations by physics-informed deep-learning models94. 
page=11 block=32900960 (x=2 y=32) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
In other circumstances where a neural network is well trained for 
page=11 block=32900960 (x=2 y=43) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the approximation of certain physical processes, transfer learning 
page=11 block=32900960 (x=2 y=53) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
can be applied to migrate the knowledge of the neural network 
page=11 block=32900960 (x=2 y=64) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
to other similar simulation scenarios with substantially reduced 
page=11 block=32900960 (x=2 y=74) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
data collection89.
page=11 block=32900960 (x=2 y=84) width=61.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Comparative advantages of deep learning over conventional opti-
page=11 block=32637564 (x=2 y=106) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
mization methods. From the viewpoint of efficiency, a well-trained 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=116) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
deep-learning model is faster by orders of magnitude than tradi-
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=127) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tional optimization algorithms46, on the premise that enough train-
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=137) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ing data are collected in advance. However, collecting data does not 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=148) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
conflict with other traditional optimization strategies9, especially 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=158) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
global optimization algorithms that extensively explore the solution 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=169) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
space. The difference between data collection for deep learning and 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=179) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
that for numerical optimization algorithms lies in how the models 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=190) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
make use of data. For deep learning, the model actively accumulates 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=200) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
training data that holistically describe the problem under investiga-
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=211) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tion, so that the model is driven by data in the sense that the quantity 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=221) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and quality of pre-collected training data contribute to its perfor-
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=232) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
mance. In contrast, traditional numerical algorithms passively pro-
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=242) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
duce data in the iterative optimization steps, where data, more like a 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=253) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
by-product, are mainly used to check how far the optimization has 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=263) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
proceeded and when the strategy needs to be changed. Usually, for a 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=274) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
specific task, the quantity of data required by deep learning is much 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=284) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
more than produced during a conventional optimization. However, 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=295) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
data collection for deep learning is a one-time cost, as opposed to 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=305) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
an ongoing cost as in the case of ab initio numerical optimization. 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=316) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
This feature makes deep learning especially competitive in photonic 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=326) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
design tasks of periodic structures such as metamaterials or pho-
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=337) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tonic crystals, where a huge number of unit cells need to be opti-
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=347) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
mized to constitute a device or a system.
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=358) width=149.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Meanwhile,  balancing  training  time  and  run  time  for  a 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=13 y=368) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
deep-learning model requires efficient exploitation of data, prob-
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=379) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ably in an online manner where data are continuously provided as 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=389) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the model evolves according to different tasks. Traditional optimi-
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=400) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
zation methods explore the design space guided by gradient infor-
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=410) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
mation (for example, level-set methods) or evolution process (for 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=421) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
example, genetic algorithm). To some extent, deep learning has 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=431) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
similarities with  gradient-based optimization  methods,  because 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=442) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the optimization of the deep-learning model is also directed by the 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=452) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
average gradient of the loss function with respect to all the training 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=463) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
data. On the other hand, similar to gradient-free optimization that 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=473) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
introduces more stochasticity to enlarge the possible design space, 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=484) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
deep learning uses a large amount of pre-collected training data to 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=494) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
extend the scope of design varieties far beyond what is achievable 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=505) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
with optimization methods.
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=515) width=103.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
In conjunction with traditional numerical optimizations, a fast 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=13 y=526) width=240.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
deep-learning approximator for the following optimization steps 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=536) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
can be constructed by saving the simulation data right from the 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=547) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
beginning of the optimization95,96. In these cases, the deep neural 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=557) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
network is also capturing the gradients of the objective functions 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=568) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
with respect to the input parameters. If a network is sufficiently 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=578) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
reliable, performing local gradient descent optimization with gra-
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=589) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
dients calculated from the surrogate network is equivalent to the 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=599) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
traditional adjoint methods. On the other hand, deep learning can 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=610) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
help to identify a global minimum in various ways. For example, 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=620) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
fast inference speed enables global search algorithms for the design 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=631) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of  highly  complex  structures.  Latent  variable  models and  gen-
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=641) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
erative models are able to capture the underlying physics so that 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=652) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
unnecessary exploration of the solution space can be avoided. In 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=662) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
more  practical  cases,  traditional  optimization  and  data-driven 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=673) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
methods can be jointly incorporated into the same design pipe-
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=683) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
line, where deep learning serves to identify solutions near global 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=694) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
minimum and traditional optimization refines the performance to 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=704) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the extreme. Even if a true global optimum can hardly be found 
page=11 block=32969252 (x=2 y=715) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
in a complex photonic design task, these optimization methods, 
page=11 block=31264576 (x=263 y=32) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
including deep learning, always yield fairly good results beyond 
page=11 block=31264576 (x=263 y=43) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
empirical designs. In some cases in which only a small amount of 
page=11 block=31264576 (x=263 y=53) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
data can be collected in a reasonable time, incorporating statisti-
page=11 block=31264576 (x=263 y=64) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
cal learning and other machine-learning algorithms to the design 
page=11 block=31264576 (x=263 y=74) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
strategy is also advantageous. However, because deep-learning and 
page=11 block=31264576 (x=263 y=85) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
machine-learning models always require sufficient training datas-
page=11 block=31264576 (x=263 y=95) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ets, data-driven approaches may not be an effective starting point 
page=11 block=31264576 (x=263 y=106) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
in extreme instances where retrieving  a single data point takes 
page=11 block=31264576 (x=263 y=116) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
from hours to days. Instead, direct optimization such as Bayesian 
page=11 block=31264576 (x=263 y=127) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
optimization could  be  considered  before  deep-learning  models 
page=11 block=31264576 (x=263 y=137) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
are applied.
page=11 block=31264576 (x=263 y=148) width=42.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Enlarging design space for ‘global’ photonic designs. Addressing 
page=11 block=31261686 (x=263 y=169) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
modern multidisciplinary  fundamental  science and  engineering 
page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=179) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
challenges, such as those related to energy, security, data process-
page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=190) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ing and storage, as well as emerging quantum technology, requires 
page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=200) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
multifunctional devices with highly constrained electromagnetic, 
page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=211) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
chemical,  thermal  and mechanical properties.  Thus far,  optical 
page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=221) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
technologies have mainly used photonic optimization in a restricted 
page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=232) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
design space that is largely limited to structural topology (geom-
page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=242) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
etry) and shape. Such optimization often omits in-depth feedback 
page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=253) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
from other critical design layers including the evolution of electro-
page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=263) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
magnetic properties with time or in harsh environments, as well as 
page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=274) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
fabrication and characterization constraints. This represents a fun-
page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=284) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
damental limitation, which prevents current optimization meth-
page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=295) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ods from providing efficient, ‘globally’ optimized solutions. For 
page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=305) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
example, global optimization would be critical for multiparametric 
page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=316) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and multiscale optimization of structures based on nanophotonic, 
page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=326) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
phase-change,  nonlinear and gain material  platforms, in which 
page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=337) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
optical response depends strongly on material composition and the 
page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=347) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
fabrication process. Further development of advanced hybrid opti-
page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=358) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
mization schemes will be a key step to address such optimization 
page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=368) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
challenges and seek potential answers for the ultimate achievable 
page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=379) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
performance of a photonic device.
page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=389) width=126.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
By  integrating  available  material  knowledge  into  the  train-
page=11 block=31357690 (x=274 y=400) width=238.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ing process, latent space could be extended to include a rich con-
page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=410) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
stituent  material  domain  into  the  compressed  representation.

For instance, owing to temperature-dependent dielectric permit-
page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=431) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tivity of the refractory material platforms97, it is challenging to 
page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=441) width=251.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
design  metasurface-based  thermal  emitters  for  thermophoto-
page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=452) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
voltaics that operate  efficiently in a wide temperature range. If

temperature-dependent dielectric permittivity functions of the con-

stituent material platform are incorporated into the training process,

a ‘globally’ optimized design with robust emissivity response over a 
page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=494) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
wide range of operating temperatures may be determined. Global 
page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=505) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
optimization search within the extended compressed space will not

only ensure optimization of the design topology but will also pro-

page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=536) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=547) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
targeted photonic device, architecture and system. This approach 
page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=557) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
could be used to address various nonlinear problems in photon-

ics, such as quantum entangled-state generation using spontane-

ous parametric down-conversion based on metastructures98. To be

page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=610) width=251.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
also the nonlinear properties of the constituent material. By inte-
page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=620) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=631) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

process, it would be possible to simultaneously optimize the topol-
page=11 block=31357690 (x=263 y=652) width=249.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=MinionPro-Regular wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ogy or shape of metastructures and retrieve the optimal deposition

parameters of the host materials.

We  envisage  that  the  global  optimization  framework  could

proper photonic concept that would give the optimal performance
